) that the class-wise error probabilities can be calculated as follows:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e|c}=\mathbb{P}\Big\{\widehat{Y}\neq Y|Y=c\Big\}=\frac{1}{\bm{\pi}_{c}}\,\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\bm{h}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0}_{k},\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\mu},\bm{\mu}})}\left[\frac{e^{\bm{h}_{c}}}{{\sum_{\ell\in[k]}e^{\bm{h}_{\ell}}}}\left(1-\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{{\bm{z}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{k-1})}\left\{\bm{S}_{c}^{1/2}{\bm{z}}\geq\bm{t}_{c}(\bm{h})\right\}\right)\right],

(D.7)

where
\bm{\pi}_{c}
is the
c^{\text{th}}
entry of the vector
\bm{\pi}
in (
4.1
) and
\bm{S}_{c},\bm{t}_{c}(\bm{h})
are defined in (
D.6
).

D.4
Evaluating and bounding tail probabilities of multivariate Gaussians

In Sections
D.3
and
D.2
, we expressed the class-wise probability of missclassification error for both GMM and MLM in the following convenient form for
{\bm{z}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{k-1})
,

\displaystyle 1-\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{{\bm{A}}^{1/2}{\bm{z}}\leq\bm{t}\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\bigcup_{i\in[k-1]}\{\widetilde{\bm{a}}_{i}^{T}{\bm{z}}\geq\bm{t}_{i}\}\right\}\,.

(D.8)

Here,
{\bm{A}}\succeq\bm{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)\times(k-1)},\bm{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{k-1}
are appropriate coefficient matrices (see (
D.4
) and (
D.5
)) and
\widetilde{\bm{a}}_{i}
denotes the
i
th row of the matrix
{\bm{A}}^{1/2}
. For example, (
D.8
) maps to (
D.4
) for
{\bm{A}}\leftarrow\bm{S}_{c}
and
\bm{t}\leftarrow(-\bm{t}_{c})
.

The formulation above is convenient both in our theoretical analysis, as well as, in simulations. In the rest of this section, we briefly discuss some relevant tools that allow to further simplify or bound expressions in the form of (
D.8
).

D.4.1
A special case: Rank-one update of Identity

First, we discuss the case where the coefficient matrix
{\bm{A}}
and vector
\bm{t}
in (
D.8
) take the special form
{\bm{A}}\propto{\bm{I}}+\bm{1}\bm{1}^{T}
and
\bm{t}\propto\bm{1}
. This special case appears in some of the stylized symmetric problem settings studied in this paper, such as classification problems with orthogonal and equally-balanced means.

Lemma D.1

Let
{\bm{A}}={\bm{I}}_{k}+\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}
and
{\bm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{A}})
. Then, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle 1-\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{{\bm{g}}\leq t\bm{1}_{k}\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{G_{0}+\max_{i\in[k]}G_{i}\geq t\},~~G_{0},G_{1},\ldots,G_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,.

(D.9)

Proof

For each
i\in[n]
, we can decompose

{\bm{g}}_{i}=G_{0}+G_{i},

where
G_{0},G_{1},\ldots,G_{k}
are iid standard normals. Indeed, it can be readily checked from this that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{i}^{2}]=2
and
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{i}{\bm{g}}_{j}]=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[G_{0}^{2}]=1,~i\neq j
, which is consistent with
{\bm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{A}})
.
Thus, we can write

1-\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{{\bm{g}}\leq t\bm{1}_{k}\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{\max_{i\in[k]}{\bm{g}}_{i}\geq t\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{G_{0}+\max_{i\in[k]}G_{i}\geq t\},

which completes the proof.

D.4.2
Slepian’s bound

When the matrix
{\bm{A}}
does not have the special structure assumed by Lemma
D.1
, it is not possible in general to provide simple expressions as the one in (
D.9
). Yet, it might be possible to obtain upper bounds of the same simple form. Such simple bounds can be useful for theoretical interpretations of otherwise complicated formulae, or can provide efficient means for quick (but, non-tight) implementations.

In this section, we discuss Slepian’s inequality (see
C.1
) as a useful tool in this direction.
Assume that

a=\min_{i,j\in[k]}{\bm{A}}_{ij}\geq 0.

To begin, note that

{\bm{A}}\geq\left({\rm diag}({\bm{A}})-a{\bm{I}}\right)\,+a\bm{1}\bm{1}^{T},

where the inequality holds element-wise and equality is true for the diagonal elements. Then, one can apply Slepian’s Lemma
C.1
to upper bound the conditional probability of error in (
D.8
) with the following simple bound:

\displaystyle 1-\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{{\bm{A}}^{1/2}{\bm{g}}\leq\bm{t}\}

\displaystyle\leq 1-\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left(\left({\rm diag}({\bm{A}})-a{\bm{I}}\right)\,+a\bm{1}\bm{1}^{T}\right)^{1/2}{\bm{g}}\leq\bm{t}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big\{\bigcup_{j\in[k]}\big\{\,G_{0}+G_{j}\sqrt{{[{\bm{A}}]_{jj}}/{a}-1}\geq{[\bm{t}]_{j}}/{a}\,\big\}\Big\},\qquad G_{0},G_{1},\ldots,G_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,.

In the second line above, we used the Gaussian decomposition of Lemma
D.1
.

D.4.3
Simple bounds for GMM

Union bound.
Of course, it is also possible to apply (a simpler) union bound to upper bound the tail probability in (
D.8
). Here, we show explicitly the result of applying union bound to the class-wise error probabilities of the GMM. Specifically, consider (
D.1
). An application of the union bound leads to the following:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e|c}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists j\neq c~:~\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j},{\bm{z}}\rangle\leq\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c},\bm{\mu}_{c}\rangle+(\widehat{\bm{b}}_{j}-\widehat{\bm{b}}_{c})\right)

(D.10)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\neq c}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}\left\{\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}G\leq\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c},\bm{\mu}_{c}\rangle+(\widehat{\bm{b}}_{j}-\widehat{\bm{b}}_{c})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\neq c}Q\left(\langle\frac{\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}}{\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}},\bm{\mu}_{c}\rangle+\frac{\widehat{\bm{b}}_{c}-\widehat{\bm{b}}_{j}}{\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\neq c}Q\left(\frac{-[\bm{t}_{c}]_{j}}{\sqrt{[\bm{S}_{c}]_{jj}}}\right)

(D.11)

\displaystyle\leq(k-1)\cdot Q\left(d_{\min}\right),

where in (
D.11
)
\bm{S}_{c},\bm{t}_{c}
are defined in (
D.3
) and in the last line we denote
d_{\min}:=\min_{j\neq c}\big\{{-[\bm{t}_{c}]_{j}}\Big/{\sqrt{[\bm{S}_{c}]_{j,j}}}\big\}.

Union bound without knowledge of cross-correlations
\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{w}_{j}\rangle,~i\neq j
.
It is worth noting that the upper bound in (
D.11
) requires knowledge of the cross-correlations
\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\rangle,~j\neq c
, i.e., of the off-diagonal entries of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{w}}
. Thankfully, our analysis allows predicting these values. For comparison, we ask wether it is possible to further upper bound the class-wise error probability if only the diagonal entries of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{w}}
(i.e., the norms
\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}},j\in[k]
) were known. A simple answer to this questions is as follows. Observe that
\sqrt{[\bm{S}_{c}]_{jj}}=\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\leq\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}.
Thus, if
[\bm{t}_{c}]_{j}<0
then the
j
th term in (
D.11
) is further upper bounded by
Q\left(\frac{-[\bm{t}_{c}]_{j}}{\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right)
:

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e|c}\leq\begin{cases}\sum_{j\neq c}Q\left(\frac{-[\bm{t}_{c}]_{j}}{\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right)&\text{if }\bm{t}_{c}\leq\bm{0},\\
1&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Unfortunately, this bound becomes non-trivial for the class-wise probability of error only if

[\bm{t}_{c}]_{j}<0\Leftrightarrow\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c},\bm{\mu}_{c}\rangle+\widehat{\bm{b}}_{c}\geq\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j},\bm{\mu}_{c}\rangle+\widehat{\bm{b}}_{j}\text{ for all }j\neq c.

Intuitively, this assumes a regime wherethe weight vector
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}
corresponding to class
c
aligns better with the corresponding mean vector
\bm{\mu}_{c}
than the rest of the weight vectors
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j},~j\neq c.

This emphasizes the important role of the cross-correlation matrix
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{w}}
(including the off-diagonals) for accurate performance prediction. For an illustration, we have implemented this bound and have compared it to our sharp predictions in Figure
7
.

Oracle lower bound.
For completeness, we briefly discuss an oracle lower bound for the class-wise probability of error in GMM. Specifically, assume that the means
\bm{\mu}_{i},~i\in[n]
are known. Then the problem of classifying a new sample
\bm{x}
is a
k
-ary hypothesis testing problem with Gaussian conditionals. Denote
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rm genie,Bayes}
the Bayes error of this hypothesis testing problem. Clearly
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rm genie,Bayes}
is a lower bound on the error of any classifier that is trained on data. The paper
[
WBW+16
]
further lower bounds
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rm genie,Bayes}
in terms of the Bayesian probability of errors between every two classes as follows:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e}

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rm genie,Bayes}\geq\frac{2}{k}\sum_{i\neq j}\pi_{i}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rm genie,ij}

(D.12)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{k}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{i}+\pi_{j}}\Big\{\pi_{i}\cdot Q\left(\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}_{i}-\bm{\mu}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}{2}+\frac{\log(\pi_{i}/\pi_{j})}{\left\|\bm{\mu}_{i}-\bm{\mu}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right)+\pi_{j}\cdot Q\left(\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}_{i}-\bm{\mu}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}{2}-\frac{\log(\pi_{i}/\pi_{j})}{\left\|\bm{\mu}_{i}-\bm{\mu}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right)\Big\}.

where
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rm genie,ij}
is the Bayesian error between classes
i
and
j
with priors
\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{i}+\pi_{j}}
and
\frac{\pi_{j}}{\pi_{i}+\pi_{j}}
. For the last equality we have used the well-known formula for the Bayesian probability of binary Gaussian hypothesis testing. In the case of equal-priors the genie lower bound above simplifies to

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e}\geq\frac{2}{k}\sum_{i\neq j}{\pi_{i}}\cdot Q\left(\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}_{i}-\bm{\mu}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}{2}\right).

(D.13)

Note that, in contrast to (
D.13
), our analysis allows for precise evaluations of the missclassification error
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e}
.

Appendix E
The Class-averaging estimator

E.1
Proofs for GMM

E.1.1
GMM: Proof of Proposition
3.1

The first statement (
3.1a
) follows directly from the fact that
\frac{1}{n}\bm{1}^{T}{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\frac{n_{i}}{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi_{i}.
For the next two statements note that

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{i}=\frac{1}{n}{\bm{M}}{\bm{Y}}{\bm{Y}}_{i}+\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z}{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bm{\mu}_{j}({\bm{Y}}_{j}^{T}{\bm{Y}}_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z}{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\frac{\left\|{\bm{Y}}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{n}\bm{\mu}_{i}+\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z}{\bm{Y}}_{i},

(E.1)

where in the last line we used orthogonality of the rows
{\bm{Y}}_{j}
of the matrix
{\bm{Y}}
:

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{Y}}_{i},{\bm{Y}}_{j}\rangle=0,\forall i\neq j\in[k].

(E.2)

To conclude simply use the facts that for all
i\in[k]
:

(i)

\frac{\left\|{\bm{Y}}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{n}=\frac{n_{i}}{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi_{i}
.

(ii)

\bm{Z}{\bm{Y}}_{i}\sim\sigma\left\|{\bm{Y}}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}{\bm{g}}_{i}
with
{\bm{g}}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{d})
because of (
E.2
).

(iii)

\frac{\left\|{\bm{g}}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}{\sqrt{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sqrt{\gamma}
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\langle{\bm{g}}_{i},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
.

E.2
Proofs for MLM

E.2.1
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Let us define
{\bm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{r})
and random vector

\bm{v}=[V_{1},V_{2},\ldots,V_{k}]^{T}
with entries:

\displaystyle\bm{v}=\frac{e^{{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}{\bm{1}_{k}^{T}e^{{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}},~~~~~~V_{i}=\frac{e^{\bm{e}_{i}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\bm{e}_{j}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}},~i\in[k].

(E.3)

We will prove the following three statements:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bm{v}\right]

(E.4a)

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{\mu}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bm{v}{\bm{g}}^{T}\right]\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}

(E.4b)

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{w}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\gamma\cdot\text{diag}(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bm{v}\right])+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bm{v}{\bm{g}}^{T}\right]\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\bm{g}}\bm{v}^{T}\right].

(E.4c)

These lead to (
4.2
) using Lemma
C.3
. Therefore, in what follows, we prove (
E.4
)

For the intercepts
\widehat{\bm{b}}_{\ell},~\ell\in[k]
it holds that

\widehat{\bm{b}}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\bm{1}^{T}{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\{Y=\ell\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{e^{\bm{h}_{\ell}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\bm{h}_{j}}}\right],

where
\bm{h}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\bm{0}_{k},\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}}\right)
. To deduce the first statement in (
E.4a
), note that
\bm{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm(D)}}{{=\joinrel=}}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}
.

Continuing with the vectors
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},~\ell\in[k]
, recall that
\bm{w}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{n}{\bm{X}}{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i_{\ell}\in[n]}\bm{x}_{i_{\ell}}[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i_{\ell}}
. Consider the singular decomposition

\bm{M}=\bm{U}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{V}^{T}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{u}}_{1}&{\bm{u}}_{2}&\ldots&{\bm{u}}_{r}\end{bmatrix}\text{diag}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{r})\begin{bmatrix}\bm{v}^{T}_{1}\\
\bm{v}_{2}^{T}\\
\ldots\\
\bm{v}_{r}^{T}\end{bmatrix},

with
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
,
\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, and
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times r}
where
r=
rank
(\bm{M})\leq k
. Decompose
{\bm{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
as
{\bm{X}}={\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{T}{\bm{X}}+{\bm{P}}^{\perp}{\bm{X}}
with
{\bm{P}}^{\perp}={\bm{I}}_{d}-{\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{T}.
With this notation we compute

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{\ell},\bm{\mu}_{c}\rangle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bm{x}^{T}_{i}\bm{\mu}_{c}[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\bm{x}^{T}_{i}{\bm{u}}_{j})\cdot(\bm{\mu}_{c}^{T}{\bm{u}}_{j})[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\cdot(\bm{\mu}_{c}^{T}{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i})[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\bm{e}_{c}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{j}\right)\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\bm{g}}_{j}\frac{\left(e^{\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}\right)}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}e^{\bm{e}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}{\bm{g}}_{j}]\,\left(\bm{e}_{j}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{e}_{c}\right).

(E.5)

Here, we have recognized that for every
i\in[n]:{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}\sim{\bm{g}}
, and also, conditioned on
\bm{x}_{i}
:
[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}\sim{\rm Bern}\left(e^{\bm{\mu}_{\ell}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}}\big/\sum_{\ell^{\prime}}e^{\bm{\mu}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}}\right)
and
\bm{\mu}_{\ell}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}=\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}\sim\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}},\ell\in[k]
.
This shows the second statement in (
E.4b
) when expressed in matrix form.

We proceed similarly with the proof of the last statement in (
E.4c
) as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{\ell},\bm{w}_{c}\rangle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i_{\ell}\in[n],i_{c}\in[n]}\bm{x}^{T}_{i_{\ell}}\bm{x}_{i_{c}}[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i_{\ell}}[{\bm{Y}}_{c}]_{i_{c}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i_{\ell}\in[n],i_{c}\in[n]}(\bm{x}^{T}_{i_{\ell}}{\bm{u}}_{j})(\bm{x}^{T}_{i_{c}}{\bm{u}}_{j})[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i_{\ell}}[{\bm{Y}}_{c}]_{i_{c}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i_{\ell}\in[n],i_{c}\in[n]}\left({\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i_{\ell}}\right)^{T}\left({\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i_{c}}\right)[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i_{\ell}}[{\bm{Y}}_{c}]_{i_{c}}

For
i_{\ell}=i_{c}=i\in[n]
note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\sum_{j=1}^{r}(\bm{x}^{T}_{i}{\bm{u}}_{j})(\bm{x}^{T}_{i}{\bm{u}}_{j})[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}[{\bm{Y}}_{c}]_{i}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

while, for
i_{\ell}\neq i_{c}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i_{\ell}\neq i_{c}\in[n]}\sum_{j=1}^{r}(\bm{x}^{T}_{i_{\ell}}{\bm{u}}_{j})[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i_{\ell}}(\bm{x}^{T}_{i_{c}}{\bm{u}}_{j})[{\bm{Y}}_{c}]_{i_{c}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\bm{g}}_{j}\cdot\frac{\left(e^{\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}\right)}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}e^{\bm{e}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}\right]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\bm{g}}_{j}\cdot\frac{\left(e^{\bm{e}_{c}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}\right)}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}e^{\bm{e}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}{\bm{g}}_{j}]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{c}]=\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{E}}[\bm{v}{\bm{g}}^{T}]\cdot{\bm{E}}[{\bm{g}}\bm{v}^{T}]\bm{e}_{c}^{T}

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}[{\bm{Y}}_{c}]_{i}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\gamma\cdot\mathds{1}_{\ell,c}\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\frac{e^{\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}e^{\bm{e}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}\right)\right]=\gamma\cdot\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}\text{diag}(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\bm{v}])\bm{e}_{c}.

Combining the last two displays results in (
E.4b
), as desired.

E.2.2
Orthogonal means

Here, we specialize the general result of Proposition
4.1
to the special case of orthogonal means:
\langle\bm{\mu}_{i},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle=0,\forall i\neq j
. Recall the notation
\mu_{i}=\left\|\bm{\mu}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}},i\in[k]
. Then, in this case the parameters in (
4.1
) are simply given by the following

\displaystyle\bm{\pi}_{i}:=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\frac{e^{\mu_{i}G_{i}}}{\sum_{\ell\in[k]}e^{\mu_{\ell}G_{\ell}}}\big],i\in[k]\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{\Pi}_{ij}:=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\frac{e^{\mu_{i}G_{i}}e^{\mu_{j}G_{j}}}{\left(\sum_{\ell\in[k]}e^{\mu_{\ell}G_{\ell}}\right)^{2}}\big],~i,j\in[k].

(E.6)

Specifically, (
4.3
) can be equivalently expressed as

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e,\rm Avg}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\big(\arg\max_{\ell\in[k]}\left\{\gamma\cdot\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})\cdot\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}+\left(\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})-\bm{\Pi}\right)\cdot\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}\right\}\neq Y({\bm{g}})\big)

(E.7)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\big(\bigcup_{j\neq Y}\left\{\gamma\cdot\bm{\pi}_{\ell}\cdot\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}_{\ell}\geq\gamma\cdot\bm{\pi}_{Y}\cdot\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}_{Y}+(\bm{e}_{Y}-\bm{e}_{\ell})^{T}\left(\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})-\bm{\Pi}\right)\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}+(\bm{\pi}_{Y}-\bm{\pi}_{\ell})\right\}\big),

(E.8)

where
{\bm{g}},\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{k})
,
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y({\bm{g}})=c\right)=\frac{e^{\mu_{c}{\bm{g}}_{c}}}{\sum_{\ell\in[k]}e^{\mu_{\ell}{\bm{g}}_{\ell}}}
and
\bm{\Sigma}=\text{diag}(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k})
.

Appendix F
On the Bayes risk of GMM: Proof of Proposition
3.4

Without loss of generality in this proof we assume
\sigma=1
. The general result follows by simply replacing
(\mu,\sigma)
with
(\frac{\mu}{\sigma},1)
and using the proof for
\sigma=1
. Recall that the feature vectors
\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
of the training data set are given by:

\bm{x}_{i}={\bm{M}}\bm{y}_{i}+{\bm{z}}_{i},\quad i\in[n],

where the matrix of means
{\bm{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
has iid Gaussian entries with variance
\mu^{2}/d
,
{\bm{z}}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{d})
and
\bm{y}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\rm{Unif}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)
with
\mathbf{e}_{j}
denoting the
j^{\text{t}h}
canonical vector in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. By definition here the Bayes estimator is the maximum-likelihood (ML) estimator. By applying the law of total probability and by successive application of the Bayes rule we have the following chain of reformulations of the ML:

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{y}}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},~j\in[k]}P\left\{\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j}~|~{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}},\bm{x}_{n+1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},~j\in[k]}\int P\left\{\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j}~|~{\bm{M}},{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}},\bm{x}_{n+1}\right\}P\left\{{\bm{M}}~|~{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}},\bm{x}_{n+1}\right\}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},~j\in[k]}\int\frac{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}},{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}\cdot P\left\{\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j}~|~{\bm{M}},{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}}{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}|{\bm{M}},{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}}P\left\{{\bm{M}}~|~{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}},\bm{x}_{n+1}\right\}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},~j\in[k]}\int{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}}\right\}}\,\frac{P\left\{{\bm{M}}~|~{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}},\bm{x}_{n+1}\right\}}{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}|{\bm{M}},{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}

(F.1)

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},~j\in[k]}\int{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}}\right\}}\,\frac{P\left\{{\bm{M}}~|~{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}}{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}|{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},~j\in[k]}\int{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}}\right\}}\,{P\left\{{\bm{M}}~|~{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}

(F.2)

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},~j\in[k]}\int{P\left\{\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}}\right\}}\,{P\left\{{\bm{X}}~|~{\bm{M}},{\bm{Y}}\right\}}\,P\{{\bm{M}}\}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{M}}

(F.3)

To arrive in (
F.1
) we used that
P\left(\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j}~|~{\bm{M}},{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right)=\pi,~\forall j\in[k]
and
P\left(\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}},{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}\right)=P\left(\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}}\right)
. Also, (
F.2
) follows by recognizing that
P(\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}})>0
is independent of the variable of integration
{\bm{M}}
and of the optimization variable
j
. For the same reasons, in (
F.3
) we have ignored the normalizing term
P({\bm{X}}|{\bm{Y}})
.

Recalling that
{\bm{z}}_{n+1}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{d})
, we have that
P\left(\bm{x}_{n+1}~|~\bm{y}=\bm{e}_{j},{\bm{M}}\right)\propto\exp\left(-\left\|\bm{x}_{n+1}-\bm{\mu}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\big/2\right)
where
\propto
hides constant positive terms.
Moreover, the posterior probability of the mean matrix given the training data is given by

\displaystyle P\left({\bm{X}}~|~{\bm{M}},{\bm{Y}}\right)\cdot P\left({\bm{M}}\right)

\displaystyle\propto\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{X}}-{\bm{M}}{\bm{Y}}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right)\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{M}}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}/d)}\right)

\displaystyle\propto\prod_{c=1}^{k}\left\{\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}/d)}\right)\cdot\prod_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{c}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{\mu}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right)\right\},

(F.4)

where we denote by
\mathcal{C}_{c}
the collection of training samples that belong to class
c\in[k]
, i.e.
\mathcal{C}_{c}=\{i\in[n]~|~\bm{y}_{i}=\bm{e}_{c}\}.

With these the objective function of the ML rule in (
F.3
) becomes:

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{y}}_{n+1}=\arg\max_{j\in[k]}~{\mathcal{I}}(j,\mathcal{C}_{j}\cap\{n+1\})\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}c=1\\
c\neq j\end{subarray}}^{k}{\mathcal{I}}(c,\mathcal{C}_{c})\,,

(F.5)

where for
\ell\in[k]
and a subset
\mathcal{A}\subset[n+1]
we denote

{\mathcal{I}}(\ell,\mathcal{A}):=\int\mathrm{d}{\bm{\mu}_{\ell}}\,\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|\bm{\mu}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}/d)}\right)\cdot\exp\left(-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\left\|\bm{x}_{i}-\bm{\mu}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right)\,.

By completing the squares and invoking a gaussian integral it can be shown that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}(\ell,\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\frac{(d/\mu^{2}+|\mathcal{A}|)}{(2{\varpi})^{d}}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{d/\mu^{2}+|\mathcal{A}|}\right)}{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\left(d/\mu^{2}+|\mathcal{A}|\right)}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\mathcal{A}\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{j}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\frac{(d/\mu^{2}+|\mathcal{A}|)}{(2{\varpi})^{d}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2\left(\frac{d}{\mu^{2}}+{|\mathcal{A}|}\right)}\left(\left(\frac{d}{\mu^{2}}+|\mathcal{A}|-1\right)\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\mathcal{A}\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{j}\rangle\right)\right)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\frac{(d/\mu^{2}+|\mathcal{A}|)}{(2{\varpi})^{d}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2\left(\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+{|\mathcal{A}|/n}\right)}\left(\left(\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{|\mathcal{A}|}{n}-\frac{1}{n}\right)\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\mathcal{A}\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{j}\rangle\right)\right).

Using this in (
F.5
) we have that

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{y}}_{n+1}=\arg\max_{j\in[k]}~{\mathcal{I}}(j)\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2\left(\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{n_{j}+1}{n}\right)}\left(\left(\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{n_{j}}{n}\right)\left\|\bm{x}_{n+1}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\frac{2}{n}\langle\bm{x}_{n+1},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{\ell}\rangle\right)\right),

(F.6)

where
\xi(n_{c}):=\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{n_{c}}{n},~c\in[k]
and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}(j):=\left\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}c=1\\
c\neq j\end{subarray}}^{k}e^{-\frac{1}{2\xi(n_{c})}\left(\left(\xi(n_{c})-\frac{1}{n}\right)\sum_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{c}}\left\|\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{c}}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{c}\\
\ell\neq i\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{\ell}\rangle\right)}\right\}\cdot e^{-\frac{1}{2\left(\xi(n_{j})+\frac{1}{n}\right)}\left(\xi(n_{j})\sum_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\left\|\bm{x}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}\\
\ell\neq i\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{\ell}\rangle\right)}\,.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{y}}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{j\in[k]}~\log\left({\mathcal{I}}(j)\right)-\frac{1}{2\left(\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{n_{j}+1}{n}\right)}\left\{\left(\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{n_{j}}{n}\right)\left\|\bm{x}_{n+1}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\frac{2}{n}\langle\bm{x}_{n+1},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{\ell}\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{j\in[k]}~\log\left({\mathcal{I}}(j)\right)+\frac{1}{2\left(\frac{d/n}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{n_{j}+1}{n}\right)}\left\{\frac{2}{n}\langle\bm{x}_{n+1},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}\end{subarray}}\bm{x}_{\ell}\rangle\right\}.

(F.7)

Next, we evaluate the objective in (
F.7
) in the asymptotic limit
n,d\rightarrow\infty,n/d=\gamma
. First, since
n_{c}/n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi
, note that
{\mathcal{I}}(j)-{\mathcal{I}}(\ell)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
for all
\ell,j\in[k]
. Moreover, note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\langle\bm{x}_{n+1},\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\bm{x}_{\ell}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\langle{\bm{M}}\bm{y}_{n+1}+{\bm{z}}_{n+1},n_{j}\,\bm{\mu}_{j}+\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{j}}{n}\langle{\bm{M}}\bm{y}_{n+1},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle+\frac{n_{j}}{n}\langle{\bm{z}}_{n+1},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\langle{\bm{M}}\bm{y}_{n+1},{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\rangle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\langle{\bm{z}}_{n+1},{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\rangle

(F.8)

For each one of the four terms in (
F.8
), we have the following by the CLT:

\displaystyle\frac{n_{j}}{n}\langle{\bm{M}}\bm{y}_{n+1},\bm{\mu}_{\ell}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi\mu^{2}\langle\bm{y}_{n+1},\bm{e}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle\frac{n_{j}}{n}\langle{\bm{z}}_{n+1},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{(D)}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,\pi^{2}r^{2})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\langle{\bm{M}}\bm{y}_{n+1},{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\langle{\bm{z}}_{n+1},{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{(D)}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,\pi\gamma),

where in the last line we used the fact that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{j}}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}_{j}}\frac{\langle{\bm{z}}_{n+1},{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\rangle}{\sqrt{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{(D)}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,\gamma)
.

Therefore, in the asymptotic limit, the Bayes estimator is the solution to:

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{y}}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=\arg\max_{\bm{e}_{j},j\in[k]}\pi\mu^{2}\langle\bm{y}_{n+1},\bm{e}_{j}\rangle+\sqrt{\pi\left(\pi\mu^{2}+\gamma\right)}\,G_{j},\quad G_{1},\ldots,G_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(F.9)

As such, the probability of error is

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\hat{\bm{y}}_{n+1}\neq\bm{y}_{n+1}\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\pi\mu^{2}+\sqrt{\pi\left(\pi\mu^{2}+\gamma\right)}\,G_{0}\leq\max_{\ell\in[k-1]}\sqrt{\pi\left(\pi\mu^{2}+\gamma\right)}\,G_{\ell}\right\}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{G_{0}+\max_{\ell\in[k-1]}G_{\ell}\geq\mu^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{\pi\mu^{2}+\gamma}}\right\}.

(F.10)

Appendix G
Least-squares for GMM

G.1
Proof of Theorem
3.2

G.1.1
Computing
\Sigma_{w,\mu}

The LS classifier solves:

\displaystyle\underset{\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d},\text{ }\bm{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}{\min}\quad\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\bm{W}\bm{X}+\bm{b}\bm{1}_{n}^{T}-\bm{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\bm{X}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+\bm{Z}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\,.

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{PO}\left(\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}\right):=\frac{1}{2n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+\bm{Z}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\,.

(G.1)

Identifying the AO.
To continue further note that by duality we have

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\mathcal{L}_{PO}\left(\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}\right)=\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\max_{\bm{s}}\text{ }\frac{1}{n}\left(\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Z}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{s}^{T}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Y}_{\ell}-\frac{\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right)\,.

Note that the above is jointly convex in
(\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell})
and concave in
\bm{s}
and the Gaussian matrix
\bm{Z}
is independent of everything else. Thus, the objective is in the form of (
6.7a
) and so we consider the corresponding Auxiliary Optimization (AO) problem:

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\max_{\bm{s}}\text{ }\frac{1}{n}\left(\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+\sigma\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}^{T}\bm{s}+\sigma\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{h}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{s}^{T}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Y}_{\ell}-\frac{\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right),

where
\bm{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\bm{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are independent Gaussian random vectors with i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries. Maximizing over the direction of
\bm{s}
and setting its norm
\beta=\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
we arrive at

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\max_{\beta\geq 0}\text{ }

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left(\beta\left\|\sigma\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}+\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\beta\sigma\bm{h}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2n}\left(\left\|\sigma\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}+\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\sigma\bm{h}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right)_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\frac{1}{2}\left(\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sigma\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}+\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sigma\bm{h}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right)_{+}^{2}

Scalarization of the AO.

For convenience, define

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{AO,\ell}:=\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sigma\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}+\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{M}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{h}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}.

(G.2)

To continue, consider the singular value decomposition

\displaystyle\bm{M}=\bm{U}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{V}^{T}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{u}}_{1}&{\bm{u}}_{2}&\ldots&{\bm{u}}_{r}\end{bmatrix}\text{diag}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{r})\begin{bmatrix}\bm{v}^{T}_{1}\\
\bm{v}_{2}^{T}\\
\ldots\\
\bm{v}_{r}^{T}\end{bmatrix},

(G.3)

with
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
,
\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, and
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times r}
where
r=
rank
(\bm{M})\leq k
. We further decompose
\bm{w}_{\ell}
in its projections on the orthogonal columns
{\bm{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{u}}_{r}
of
{\bm{U}}
:

\bm{w}_{\ell}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}{\bm{u}}_{i}+\alpha_{0}\bm{w}_{\ell}^{\perp},

where
\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}^{\perp}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}=1
and
{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}^{\perp}=\mathbf{0}
,
\alpha_{0}\geq 0
and we denote

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}:={\bm{u}}_{i}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell},i\in[r].

(G.4)

We also define
\bm{\alpha}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{1}&\alpha_{2}&\ldots&\alpha_{k}\end{bmatrix}^{T}
.
In this notation, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{AO,\ell}({\bm{g}},\bm{h})

\displaystyle:=\min_{\alpha_{0}\geq 0,\text{ }\bm{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{r},\text{ }b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sigma\sqrt{\alpha_{0}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}\,\bm{g}+\bm{Y}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{r}{\alpha_{i}\sigma\frac{\bm{h}^{T}{\bm{u}}_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}}+\frac{\alpha_{0}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell}^{\perp}}\left(\bm{h}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle=\min_{\alpha_{0}\geq 0,\text{ }\bm{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{r},\text{ }b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sigma\sqrt{\alpha_{0}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}\,\bm{g}+\bm{Y}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{r}{\alpha_{i}\sigma\frac{\bm{h}^{T}{\bm{u}}_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}}-\alpha_{0}\sigma\frac{\left\|\bm{h}^{\perp}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}{\sqrt{n}},

(G.5)

where in the second line we denote
\bm{h}^{\perp}
the projection of
\bm{h}
onto the complement subspace of the span of
{\bm{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{u}}_{r}
and we recalled that
\left\|\bm{w}^{\perp}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}=1
and
\langle\bm{w}^{\perp}_{\ell},{\bm{u}}_{i}\rangle=0,i\in[r]
.

Convergence of the AO.
First, note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{T}\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{T}\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}+b_{\ell}^{2}+\frac{2}{n}b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}^{T}\bm{Y}^{T}\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{trace}\left(\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}\text{diag}\left(\frac{n_{1}}{n},\frac{n_{2}}{n},\ldots,\frac{n_{k}}{n}\right)\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+b_{\ell}^{2}+2b_{\ell}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{n_{1}}{n}&\frac{n_{2}}{n}&\ldots&\frac{n_{k}}{n}\end{bmatrix}\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)

Thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\text{ }\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{ }

\displaystyle\text{trace}\left(\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}\text{diag}\left(\bm{\pi}\right)\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+b_{\ell}^{2}+2b_{\ell}\bm{\pi}^{T}\left({\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\alpha}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}^{T}\left(\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\text{diag}(\bm{\pi}){\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\bm{\alpha}-2\pi_{\ell}\bm{\alpha}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{e}_{\ell}+2b_{\ell}\bm{\alpha}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{\pi}

\displaystyle+b_{\ell}^{2}-2b_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{\ell}\,.

At this point, observe that we have reduced the AO to an optimization problem over only
r+2
scalar variables. Using the law of large numbers, the fact that
\left\|\bm{h}^{\perp}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
concentrates around
\sqrt{d-r}
and
(d-r)/n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\gamma
, as well as the limit calculation above, it is not hard to see that for fixed
\alpha_{0},b_{\ell}
and
\bm{\alpha}=[\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r}]^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
, the objective function in (
G.5
) converges to the following:

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell})

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\alpha_{0}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\bm{\alpha}^{T}\left(\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{r}+\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\text{diag}(\bm{\pi}){\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\bm{\alpha}-2\bm{\alpha}^{T}\left(\pi_{\ell}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{e}_{\ell}-b_{\ell}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{\pi}\right)+b_{\ell}^{2}-2b_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\alpha_{0}\sigma\sqrt{\gamma},

(G.6)

We will show in the next paragraph that the argument inside the square-root in (
G.6
) is a convex quadratic over
(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell})
(see (
G.19
)). Thus, the function
{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell})
is jointly convex.
Using uniform convergence of convex functions over compact sets
[
AG82
, Cor.. II.1]
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{AO,\ell}({\bm{g}},\bm{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\min_{\alpha_{0}\geq 0,\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell}}{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell}).

(G.7)

Deterministic Analysis.
Here, we analyze the deterministic scalar minimization on the RHS of (
G.7
). Define

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}:=\begin{bmatrix}\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{r}+\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\text{diag}(\bm{\pi}){\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}&\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{\pi}\\
\bm{\pi}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}&1\end{bmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{c}_{\ell}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{e}_{\ell}\\
1\end{bmatrix}\,,

(G.8)

and observe that we can write

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell})=\sqrt{\alpha_{0}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\pi_{\ell}+\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\alpha}^{T}&b_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}{\bm{A}}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\alpha}\\
b_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}-2\pi_{\ell}\bm{c}_{\ell}^{T}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\alpha}\\
b_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}}-\alpha_{0}\sigma\sqrt{\gamma}\,.

(G.9)

First, note that the matrix
{\bm{A}}
is positive definite. This can be checked by computing the Schur complement of
{\bm{A}}
:

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}:=\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{r}+\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}{\bm{P}}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}:=\sigma^{2}{\bm{I}}_{r}+\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})-\bm{\pi}\bm{\pi}^{T}\right){\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\succ\bm{0}_{r\times r}.

(G.10)

Positive definiteness above holds because
{\bm{P}}:=\left(\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})-\bm{\pi}\bm{\pi}^{T}\right)\succeq\bm{0}_{k\times k}
.
Thus the term under the square-root in (
G.9
) is a strictly convex quadratic. Thus,
{\cal{D}}_{\ell}
is jointly convex in its arguments.

To simplify the RHS of (
G.7
) we proceed by minimizing
{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell})
over
(\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell})
which from Lemma (
C.3
) is equal to

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}\\
\widehat{b}_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}=\pi_{\ell}{\bm{A}}^{-1}\bm{c}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\pi_{\ell}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{I}}&\bm{0}\\
-\bm{\pi}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}^{T}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{I}}&-\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{\pi}\\
\bm{0}^{T}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{e}_{\ell}\\
1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\pi_{\ell}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{I}}&\bm{0}\\
-\bm{\pi}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}^{T}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}-\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\\
1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\pi_{\ell}\begin{bmatrix}-\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell})\\
1+\bm{\pi}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell})\end{bmatrix}\,.

(G.11)

Thus, the minimum value attained is

\displaystyle-\pi_{\ell}^{2}\begin{bmatrix}-\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}&&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}^{T}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}-\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\\
1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\pi_{\ell}^{2}\left(1+\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\right).

Using the above, (
G.7
) reduces to

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{AO,\ell}({\bm{g}},\bm{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\min_{\alpha_{0}\geq 0}\sqrt{\alpha_{0}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\pi_{\ell}-\pi_{\ell}^{2}\left(1+\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\right)}-\alpha_{0}\sigma\sqrt{\gamma}.

(G.12)

Setting the derivative with respect to
\alpha_{0}
to zero we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{0}\sigma^{2}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{0}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\pi_{\ell}-\pi_{\ell}^{2}\left(1+\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\right)}}=\sigma\sqrt{\gamma}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}}\sqrt{\pi_{\ell}\left(1-\pi_{\ell}\right)-\pi_{\ell}^{2}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)}.

(G.13)

Plugging the latter into (
G.12
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{AO,\ell}({\bm{g}},\bm{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sqrt{1-\gamma}\sqrt{\pi_{\ell}\left(1-\pi_{\ell}\right)-\pi_{\ell}^{2}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)}\,.

Asymptotic predictions.
First, from (
G.11
) the bias term converges as follows:

\widehat{b}_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi_{\ell}\left(1+\bm{\pi}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell})\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{b}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})\left(\bm{1}_{k}+\left(\bm{\pi}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}-{\bm{I}}_{k}\right){\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{\pi}\right)\,.

Recall from (
G.4
) that
\bm{\alpha}={\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}
. Thus, the correlations
\langle\bm{\mu}_{i},\bm{w}_{\ell}\rangle,~i\in[k]
converge as follows:

\displaystyle{\bm{M}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}={\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}=-{\pi_{\ell}}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right).\quad

(G.14)

Here, convergence applies element-wise to the entries of the involved random vectors. Moreover, from the analysis above we can predict the limit of the norm
\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
. For this, note that
\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}=\widehat{\alpha}_{0}^{2}+\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{T}\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)\sigma^{2}}\pi_{\ell}(1-\pi_{\ell})+\pi_{\ell}^{2}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\left(\bm{\Delta}^{-1}-\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)\sigma^{2}}{\bm{I}}_{r}\right)\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)\,.

(G.15)

G.1.2
Computing
\Sigma_{w,w}

In the previous section we used the CGMT to predict the bias
\widehat{b}_{\ell}
, the correlations
\langle\bm{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}\rangle,~i[k]
and the norm
\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
for all
\ell\in[k]
members of the multi-output classifier. Here, we show how to compute the limits of the cross-correlations
\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\rangle,\ell\neq j\in[k]
.

Lemma G.1

For
\ell\neq j\in[k]
, let
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}

\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}
be solutions to the least-squares minimization

(\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j},\widehat{b}_{\ell},\widehat{b}_{j})=\arg\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},\bm{w}_{j},b_{\ell},b_{j}}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\left\|{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}-{\bm{X}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}-b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2n}\left\|{\bm{Y}}_{j}-{\bm{X}}^{T}\bm{w}_{j}-b_{j}\bm{1}_{n}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\right\}.

Denote
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell,j}:=\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}
and
\widehat{b}_{\ell,j}:=\widehat{b}_{\ell}+\widehat{b}_{j}
. Then,
(\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell,j},\widehat{b}_{\ell,j})
is a minimizer in the following least-squares problem:

\displaystyle(\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell,j},\widehat{b}_{\ell,j})=\arg\min_{\bm{w},b}\frac{1}{2n}\left\|{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}+{\bm{Y}}_{j}-{\bm{X}}^{T}\bm{w}-b\bm{1}_{n}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\,.

(G.16)

Proof

Clearly the minimization in (
G.16
) is convex. Thus, it suffices to prove that
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}
satisfies the KKT conditions. First, by optimality of
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}
, we have that

{\bm{X}}\left({\bm{Y}}_{\ell}-{\bm{X}}^{T}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}-\widehat{b}_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}\right)=0

Similarly, for
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}
:

{\bm{X}}\left({\bm{Y}}_{j}-{\bm{X}}^{T}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}-\widehat{b}_{j}\bm{1}_{n}\right)=0.

Adding the equations on the above displays we find that

{\bm{X}}\left({\bm{Y}}_{\ell}+{\bm{Y}}_{j}-{\bm{X}}^{T}(\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell})-(\widehat{b}_{j}+\widehat{b}_{\ell})\bm{1}_{n}\right)=0.

Recognize that this coincides with the optimality condition for (
G.16
). Thus, the proof is complete.

Thanks to Lemma
G.1
, we can use the CGMT to characterize the limiting behavior of
\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
. Observe that this immediately gives the limit of
\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\rangle
since

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\rangle=\frac{\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}-\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}.

(G.17)

The analysis of (
G.16
) is very similar to that of (
G.1
); thus, most details are omitted. Similar to (
G.5
) we can relate (
G.16
) with the following AO problem:

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{AO,\ell,j}({\bm{g}},\bm{h})

\displaystyle:=\min_{\beta_{0}\geq 0,\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{r},b_{\ell,j}}\text{ }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sigma\sqrt{\beta_{0}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\beta}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}\,\bm{g}+\bm{Y}^{T}\bm{V}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}+b_{\ell,j}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}-{\bm{Y}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sigma\sum_{i=1}^{r}{\beta_{i}\frac{\bm{h}^{T}{\bm{u}}_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}}-\sigma\beta_{0}\frac{\left\|\bm{h}^{\perp}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}}{\sqrt{n}},

(G.18)

where we have decomposed

\bm{w}_{\ell,j}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\beta_{i}{\bm{u}}_{i}+\beta_{0}\bm{w}_{\ell,j}^{\perp},

with
\left\|\bm{w}_{\ell,j}^{\perp}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}=1
and
{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell,j}^{\perp}=\bm{0}_{r}
.

Using a calculation similar to the one leading to (
G.9
) we can show that (
G.18
) converges point-wise in
\beta_{0},\bm{\beta}=\begin{bmatrix}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{r}\end{bmatrix},b_{\ell,j}
to the following:

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\beta_{0},\bm{\beta},b_{\ell,j})=\sqrt{\beta_{0}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{j}+\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\beta}^{T}&b_{\ell,j}\end{bmatrix}{\bm{A}}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\beta}\\
b_{\ell,j}\end{bmatrix}-2{\bm{d}}_{\ell,j}^{T}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\beta}\\
b_{\ell,j}\end{bmatrix}}-\beta_{0}\sigma\sqrt{\gamma},

(G.19)

where
{\bm{A}}
is as in (
G.8
) and we have further defined

{\bm{d}}_{\ell,j}:=\begin{bmatrix}\pi_{\ell}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{e}_{\ell}+\pi_{j}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\bm{e}_{j}\\
\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{j}\end{bmatrix}.

Thus, similar to (
G.11
) we can compute the minimizer of the deterministic objective in (
G.19
):

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\\
\widehat{b}_{\ell,j}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}-\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\pi_{\ell}(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell})+\pi_{j}(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{j})\right)\\
\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{j}+\bm{\pi}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\pi_{\ell}(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell})+\pi_{j}(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{j})\right)\end{bmatrix}\,,

(G.20)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}}\sqrt{\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{j}-(\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{j})^{2}-\left(\pi_{\ell}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)+\pi_{j}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{j}\right)\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\pi_{\ell}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)+\pi_{j}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{j}\right)\right)},

(G.21)

where recall that
\bm{\Delta}
is as in (
G.10
).

From the CGMT, we have that

\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{0}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\beta}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}.

Combining this with the calculations above, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)\sigma^{2}}\left(\pi_{\ell}+\pi_{j}\right)\left(1-\pi_{\ell}-\pi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\pi_{\ell}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)+\pi_{j}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{j}\right)\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\left(\bm{\Delta}^{-1}-\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)\sigma^{2}}{\bm{I}}_{r}\right)\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\pi_{\ell}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)+\pi_{j}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{j}\right)\right)

(G.22)

Finally, using (
G.1.2
) and (
G.15
) in (
G.17
) it follows that

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{\ell},\bm{w}_{j}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi_{\ell}\pi_{j}\left(-\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)\sigma^{2}}+\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{\ell}\right)^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Delta}^{-1}\left(\bm{\Delta}^{-1}-\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)\sigma^{2}}{\bm{I}}_{r}\right)\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\bm{\pi}-\bm{e}_{j}\right)\right).

(G.23)

G.2
Orthogonal means

Here, we specialize the asymptotic predictions of Theorem
3.2
to the case of orthogonal means
\langle\bm{\mu}_{i},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle=0,~i\neq j
.

Corollary G.2
(Orthogonal means)

Consider the case of orthogonal means, i.e.
\langle\bm{\mu}_{i},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle=0,\forall i\neq j
and
\gamma<1
with Euclidean norms given by
\mu_{i}=\left\|\bm{\mu}_{i}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
. Define the following parameters for
i\in[k]
:

\rho_{i}:={\pi_{i}\sigma^{2}}\big/\left({\sigma^{2}+\pi_{i}\mu_{i}^{2}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\beta_{i}={\rho_{i}\sigma^{2}}\big/\Big({\sigma^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i}\rho_{i}\mu_{i}^{2}}\Big).

Then, the following asymptotic limits hold for the least-squares classifier, for all
i,j\in[k]
:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{b}}_{i}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\beta_{i}\,,\qquad\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{i},\bm{\mu}_{j}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma}(\mathbb{1}_{ij}-\beta_{i})\rho_{j}\mu_{j}\,,

(G.24a)

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{i},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma^{4}}\beta_{i}\beta_{j}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}^{2}\mu_{\ell}^{2}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{4}}\beta_{i}\rho_{j}^{2}\mu_{j}^{2}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{4}}\beta_{j}\rho_{i}^{2}\mu_{i}^{2}-\frac{\gamma\beta_{i}\rho_{j}}{(1-\gamma)\sigma^{2}}+\frac{\mathbb{1}_{ij}}{\sigma^{2}}\big(\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)}\rho_{i}+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\rho_{i}^{2}\mu_{i}^{2}\big)

(G.24b)

Furthermore, if the means have equal norms
\mu:=\mu_{i}
and the classes are balanced:
\pi_{i}=1/k,~i\in[k]
, then, setting
u_{\rm LS}:=\frac{\mu^{2}}{\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{1-\gamma}{\mu^{2}+k{\gamma\sigma^{2}}}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\big\{G_{0}+\max_{j\in[k-1]}G_{j}\geq u_{\rm LS}\big\},\quad G_{0},G_{1},\ldots,G_{k-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,.

(G.25)

Proof
This is a direct corollary of Theorem
3.2
. Indeed, (
G.24
) can be derived from (
3.3
) after substituting
{\bm{V}}={\bm{I}}_{k},\bm{\Sigma}=\text{diag}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\ldots,\mu_{k})
and some algebra steps that we omit for brevity.

Instead, we outline below how to conclude (
G.25
) from (
G.24
).
Assume that
\mu_{i}=\mu,\forall i\in[k]
and
\pi_{i}=\pi=1/k,\forall i\in[k]
. Recall from (
2.7
) that

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\text{error}\,|\,\bm{y}=\mathbf{e}_{c}\right)=1-\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\bm{S}_{c}^{{1}/{2}}\,{\bm{z}}>\bm{t}\right),

and using (
G.24
) it can be checked that

\bm{S}_{c}=\frac{\pi}{1+\pi\mu^{2}}\left(\frac{\pi\mu^{2}}{1+\pi\mu^{2}}+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\right)({\bm{I}}_{k}+\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{t}=-\frac{\pi\mu^{2}}{1+\pi\mu^{2}}\bm{1}.

Thus, setting

\displaystyle u_{\rm LS}:=\mu^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{\pi\mu^{2}+\left(\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\right)\left({1+\pi\mu^{2}}\right)}}=\mu^{2}\sqrt{\frac{1-\gamma}{\mu^{2}+\gamma/\pi}},

(G.26)

and applying Lemma (
D.1
), the probability of error is given by the advertised expression.

Appendix H
Least-squares for MLM

H.1
Proof of Theorem
4.2

H.1.1
Computing
\Sigma_{w,\mu}

Assume that
{\bm{X}},{\bm{Y}}
are generated from the MLM.

Fix any
\ell\in[k]
. The classifier parameters
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{b}}_{\ell}
minimize the following objective function

\mathcal{L}_{PO}\left(\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}\right):=\frac{1}{2n}\left\|{\bm{X}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}.

Identifying the AO.

To continue further note that by duality we have

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\mathcal{L}_{PO}\left(\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}\right)=\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\max_{\bm{s}}\text{ }\frac{1}{n}\left(\bm{s}^{T}{\bm{X}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{s}^{T}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Y}_{\ell}-\frac{\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right)\,,

(H.1)

and the optimization is jointly convex in
(\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell})
and concave in
\bm{s}
. Here, note that
{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}
depends on the Gaussian matrix
{\bm{X}}
. Thus, before applying the CGMT, we need to break this dependence as follows. Consider the singular value decomposition

\displaystyle\bm{M}=\bm{U}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{V}^{T}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{u}}_{1}&{\bm{u}}_{2}&\ldots&{\bm{u}}_{r}\end{bmatrix}\text{diag}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{r})\begin{bmatrix}\bm{v}^{T}_{1}\\
\bm{v}_{2}^{T}\\
\ldots\\
\bm{v}_{r}^{T}\end{bmatrix},

(H.2)

with
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
,
\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, and
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times r}
where
r=
rank
(\bm{M})\leq k
. For every
i\in[n]
, we decompose
\bm{x}_{i}
in its projection on the subspace spanned orthogonal columns
{\bm{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{u}}_{r}
as follows:

\bm{x}_{i}={\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{T}{\bm{X}}_{i}+{\bm{P}}^{\perp}{\bm{X}}_{i}={\bm{U}}\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{i}+{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i},

where
{\bm{P}}^{\perp}={\bm{I}}_{r}-{\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{T}
, and we denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{G}}:=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{1}&\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{2}&\ldots&\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{n}\end{bmatrix},\quad\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{i}:={\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{r},i\in[n].

(H.3)

Recalling that
\bm{x}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{d})
note that

\displaystyle\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{r})\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{i}\perp{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i}.

(H.4)

Further recall that for all
i\in[n]
, conditioned on
\bm{x}_{i}

\displaystyle[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}\sim{\rm Bern}\left(\frac{e^{\bm{\mu}_{\ell}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}}}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}e^{\bm{\mu}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}\bm{x}_{i}}}\right)\sim{\rm Bern}\left(\frac{e^{\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{i}}}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}e^{\bm{e}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{i}}}\right),

(H.5)

where we used (
H.3
) and the SVD decomposition of
{\bm{M}}
.
In this notation, we can rewrite the PO as follows:

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\max_{\bm{s}}\text{ }\frac{1}{n}\left(\bm{s}^{T}{\bm{X}}^{T}{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}+\bm{s}^{T}\widetilde{\bm{G}}^{T}{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{s}^{T}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Y}_{\ell}-\frac{\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right)

From (
H.4
) and (
H.5
) notice that
{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}
depends only on
\widetilde{\bm{G}}
and
\widetilde{\bm{G}}
is independent of
{\bm{X}}^{T}{\bm{P}}^{\perp}.
Therefore, the corresponding Auxiliary Optimization (AO) problem becomes

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\max_{\bm{s}}\text{ }\frac{1}{n}\left(\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}^{T}\bm{s}+\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{h}^{T}{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}+\bm{s}^{T}\widetilde{\bm{G}}^{T}{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{s}^{T}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{s}^{T}\bm{Y}_{\ell}-\frac{\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right),

(H.6)

where
{\bm{g}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\bm{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are iid Gaussian vectors independent of everything else.

Scalarization of the AO.

Maximizing over the direction of
\bm{s}
and denoting its norm
\beta=\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\geq 0
we arrive at

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\max_{\beta\geq 0}\text{ }

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left(\beta\left\|\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}+{\bm{G}}^{T}{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\beta\bm{h}^{T}{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2n}\left(\left\|\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}+\widetilde{\bm{G}}^{T}{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\bm{h}^{T}{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right)_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\frac{1}{2}\left(\min_{\bm{w}_{\ell},b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\bm{g}+\widetilde{\bm{G}}^{T}{\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{h}^{T}{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right)_{+}^{2}

(H.7)

In the remaining, we focus in the inner minimization above. Let us denote

\bm{a}:={\bm{U}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha_{0}=\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}.

Notice that
\bm{a}\perp{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}
and thus the orthogonal decomposition
\bm{w}_{\ell}={\bm{U}}\bm{a}+{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{w}_{\ell}
. With this observation, we can optimize over the direction of
{\bm{P}}^{T}\bm{w}_{\ell}
in (
H.7
) by aligning it with
-{\bm{P}}^{T}\bm{h}
. With this, the minimization in (
H.7
) reduces to the following

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{a},\alpha_{0}\geq 0,b_{\ell}}\text{ }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\alpha_{0}\bm{g}+\widetilde{\bm{G}}^{T}\bm{a}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}-\alpha_{0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{h}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\,.

(H.8)

Convergence of the AO.

First, we argue on point-wise convergence of the objective function in (
H.8
). Fix
\bm{a},\alpha_{0}
and
\bm{b}_{\ell}
. From the WLLN,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|{\bm{P}}^{\perp}\bm{h}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sqrt{\gamma}
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\alpha_{0}\bm{g}+\widetilde{\bm{G}}^{T}\bm{a}+b_{\ell}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{Y}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left({\alpha_{0}{\bm{g}}_{i}+\bm{a}^{T}\tilde{{\bm{g}}}_{i}+b_{\ell}-[{\bm{Y}}_{\ell}]_{i}}\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left({\alpha_{0}G_{0}+\bm{a}^{T}{\bm{g}}+\bm{b}_{\ell}-Y_{\ell}}\right)^{2}\right],

(H.9)

where the expectation is over
{\bm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0}_{r},{\bm{I}}_{r})
(with some abuse of notation) and

\displaystyle Y_{\ell}\sim{\rm Bern}(V_{\ell})\quad\text{and}\quad V_{\ell}=\frac{e^{\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}e^{\bm{e}_{\ell^{\prime}}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}.

(H.10)

Therefore, point-wise on
\bm{a},\alpha_{0}
and
\bm{b}_{\ell}
, the objective of the AO converges to

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell}):=\sqrt{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left({\alpha_{0}G_{0}+\bm{a}^{T}{\bm{g}}+\bm{b}_{\ell}-Y_{\ell}}\right)^{2}\right]}-\alpha_{0}\sqrt{\gamma}.

(H.11)

Next, with an argument based on convexity and compactness similar to that in “Convergence analysis of the AO" in Section
G
it can be argued that the convergence above is uniform. Thus,

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:koko2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\min_{\alpha_{0}\geq 0,\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell}}{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell}).

(H.12)

Deterministic analysis of the AO.

Here, we solve the deterministic minimization problem in (
H.12
). Optimization over
b_{\ell}
is straightforward. By setting

b_{\ell}=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{\ell}]=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}],

we now have to optimize

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha_{0}\geq 0,\bm{\alpha}}\sqrt{\alpha_{0}^{2}+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\bm{a}^{T}{\bm{g}}-Y_{\ell}\right)^{2}\right]-\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}}-\alpha_{0}\sqrt{\gamma}.

(H.13)

By direct differentiation and first-order optimality, we compute the optimal values as follows:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{a}}_{j}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}Y_{\ell}]=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{\ell}],~j\in[r]\,,

(H.14)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\textrm{Var}[Y_{\ell}]-\sum_{j=1}^{r}{\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}}\right)=\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]-\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{r}{\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}}\right)\,.

(H.15)

Asymptotic Predictions.
From the analysis above, we conclude with the following limits about the solution
\widehat{\bm{b}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}
of the PO:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{b}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]

(H.16a)

\displaystyle\langle\bm{\mu}_{c},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bm{e}_{c}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}V_{\ell}],\quad c\in[k]

(H.16b)

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{{\bm{g}}_{j}}V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]-\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{r}{\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}}\right)

(H.16c)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]-\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{1-2\gamma}{1-\gamma}\sum_{j=1}^{r}{\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{\ell}]\right)^{2}}\,.

(H.16d)

Recall the notation in (
4.1
). Note that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}]=\bm{\pi}_{\ell}
. Moreover, using Gaussian integration by parts Lemma
C.3
, it can be shown that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}{\bm{g}}]=\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{V}}^{T}\left(\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})-\bm{\Pi}\right)\bm{e}_{\ell}.
Using these and writing and in matrix form, we arrive at (
4.4a
).

H.1.2
Computing
\Sigma_{w,w}

Here, we prove (
4.4b
). Specifically, we compute the correlations
\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\rangle,~\ell\neq c\in[k]
by following the strategy of Section
G.1.2
. Specifically, in view of Lemma
G.1
we need to study the following PO:

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{w},\bm{b}}\text{ }\max_{\bm{s}}\text{ }\frac{1}{n}\left(\bm{s}^{T}{\bm{X}}^{T}\bm{w}+b\bm{s}^{T}\bm{1}_{n}-\bm{s}^{T}(\bm{Y}_{\ell}+{\bm{Y}}_{c})-\frac{\left\|\bm{s}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}}{2}\right)

(H.17)

which is minimized by
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}
. Thus the analysis will lead us to an asymptotic formula for
\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
. This when combined with the formulae for
\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
and
\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}
in (
H.16d
) will give the desired.

The analysis of (
H.17
) is almost identical to the analysis of (
H.1
) in the previous section. Specifically, without repeating all the details for brevity, it can be shown that the AO of (
H.17
) converges to the following (cf. (
H.11
):

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{\ell}(\alpha_{0},\bm{\alpha},b_{\ell}):=\sqrt{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left({\alpha_{0}G_{0}+\bm{a}^{T}{\bm{g}}+\bm{b}_{\ell}-Y_{\ell,c}}\right)^{2}\right]}-\alpha_{0}\sqrt{\gamma},

(H.18)

where as before
G_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),{\bm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0}_{r},{\bm{I}}_{r})
, only now (
H.10
) is modified to:

\displaystyle Y_{\ell,c}\sim{\rm Bern}(V_{c}+V_{\ell})\quad\text{and as before:}\quad V_{\ell}=\frac{e^{\bm{e}_{\ell}^{T}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}e^{\bm{e}_{\ell^{\prime}}{\bm{V}}\bm{\Sigma}{\bm{g}}}}.

(H.19)

With these, it can be shown that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell}+\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}(V_{c}+V_{\ell})]\right)^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{V_{c}+V_{\ell}}]-\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{V_{c}+V_{\ell}}]\right)^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}(V_{c}+V_{\ell})]\right)^{2}\right)

Combining this with (
H.16d
), we conclude that for
\ell\neq c\in[k]
:

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{w}}_{c}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1-2\gamma}{1-\gamma}\sum_{j=1}^{r}{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{c}]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{g}}_{j}V_{\ell}]}-\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{c}]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V_{\ell}].

(H.20)

This shows (
4.4b
) after applying Gaussian integration by parts and expressing it in matrix form; see Lemma
C.3
.

H.2
Orthogonal means and equal-energy

Here, we use Theorem
4.2
to prove that, in contrast to the GMM, in the MLM under orthogonal and equal-energy means: LS outperforms the averaging classifier for large enough sample sizes. Assuming orthogonal means of equal energy
\mu
:

\displaystyle\bm{\pi}

\displaystyle=\bm{\pi}_{1}\bm{1}_{k}={(1/k)}\bm{1}_{k},

(H.21)

\displaystyle\bm{\Pi}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\Pi}_{11}-\bm{\Pi}_{12}){\bm{I}}_{k}+\bm{\Pi}_{12}\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}~~\text{with}~~\bm{\Pi}_{12}=\frac{1-k^{2}\bm{\Pi}_{11}^{2}}{k(k-1)}~~\text{and}~~\bm{\Pi}_{11}=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\frac{e^{2\mu G_{1}}}{\left(\sum_{\ell\in[k]}e^{\mu G_{\ell}}\,\right)^{2}}\big].

Then,

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{w}}-\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{\mu}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\mu},\bm{\mu}}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{w},\bm{\mu}}^{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\cdot\left(p{\bm{I}}_{k}-q\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}\right),

(H.22)

where we defined

\displaystyle p:=\bm{\pi}_{1}-\mu^{2}(\bm{\pi}_{1}-\bm{\Pi}_{11}+\bm{\Pi}_{12})^{2}\text{ and }q:=(\bm{\pi}_{1}^{2}+\bm{\Pi}_{12}^{2}\mu^{2}k-2\mu^{2}\bm{\Pi}_{12}(\bm{\pi}_{1}-\bm{\Pi}_{11}+\bm{\Pi}_{12})).

(H.23)

Thus, similar to (
4.3
) and with the same notation,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e,\rm LS}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big\{\arg\max_{\ell\in[k]}\big\{\sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}}\cdot\left(p{\bm{I}}_{k}-q\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}+\mu\cdot\left(\text{diag}(\bm{\pi})-\bm{\Pi}\right){\bm{g}}\big\}\neq Y({\bm{g}})\Big\}.

(H.24)

In (
H.24
) (as well as in (
4.3
)), note that the matrices multiplying
\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}
and
{\bm{g}}
have all the form of a rank one update of a (scaled) identity matrix. It turns out that we can exploit this structure to simplify the formulae for the test error even further. Importantly, this lets us directly compare
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e,\rm LS}
and
P_{e,\rm Avg}
of the two classifiers. These are detailed in Section
H.3
.

H.3
Proof of Proposition
4.3

In (
E.7
) and (
H.24
), we showed the following limits for orthogonal means of equal-energy
\mu>0
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e,\rm Avg}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\big(\arg\max_{\ell\in[k]}\left\{\gamma\cdot\bm{\pi}_{1}\cdot{\bm{I}}_{k}\cdot\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}+\mu\,\left((\bm{\pi}_{1}-\bm{\Pi}_{11})\cdot{\bm{I}}_{k}+\bm{\Pi}_{12}\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)\cdot{\bm{g}}\right\}\neq Y({\bm{g}})\big)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e,\rm LS}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\arg\max_{\ell\in[k]}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}}\cdot\left(p{\bm{I}}_{k}-q\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{{\bm{g}}}+\mu\,\left((\bm{\pi}_{1}-\bm{\Pi}_{11})\cdot{\bm{I}}_{k}+\bm{\Pi}_{12}\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)\cdot{\bm{g}}\right\}\neq Y({\bm{g}})\Big),

where
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(Y({\bm{g}})=\ell)=\frac{e^{\mu{\bm{g}}_{\ell}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\mu{\bm{g}}_{j}}}
and we have further used (
H.21
) and the notation in (
H.23
).

We compare the expression on the RHS in the above display by applying Lemma
H.1
below with the following substitutions

\displaystyle{\bm{g}}\leftarrow\widetilde{{\bm{g}}},\quad\bm{h}\leftarrow{\bm{g}},\quad c(\bm{h})\leftarrow Y({\bm{g}})

\displaystyle p_{2}\leftarrow\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\bm{\pi}_{1}-\mu^{2}(\bm{\pi}_{1}-\bm{\Pi}_{11}+\bm{\Pi}_{12})^{2}\right),\quad q_{2}\leftarrow\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}(\bm{\pi}_{1}^{2}+\bm{\Pi}_{12}^{2}\mu^{2}k-2\mu^{2}\bm{\Pi}_{12}(\bm{\pi}_{1}-\bm{\Pi}_{11}+\bm{\Pi}_{12})),

\displaystyle p_{1}\leftarrow\gamma\bm{\pi}_{1},\quad\quad q_{1}\leftarrow 0.

This shows that with probability 1,
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e,\rm LS}<\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{e,\rm Avg}
if and only if
p_{2}<p_{1}~\Leftrightarrow~\gamma<\gamma_{\star}=\mu^{2}\left(\bm{\pi}_{1}-\bm{\Pi}_{11}+\bm{\Pi}_{12}\right)^{2}\big/\bm{\pi}_{1}
. To retrieve (
), recall that
\bm{\pi}_{1}=1/k
and
k\bm{\Pi}_{11}+(k^{2}-k)\bm{\Pi}_{12}=1
. The only thing left to prove is that
\gamma_{\star}<1
. To see this note that
p_{2}>0
from positive semi-definiteness of the Schur matrix in (
H.23
). It takes simple algebra to conclude that
p_{2}>0\implies\gamma_{\star}<1
.

Lemma H.1

Let
k\geq 2
,
{\bm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{k})

\bm{h}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{k}\right)
,
and discrete random variable
c(\bm{h})
such that
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(c(\bm{h})=\ell)=e^{\bm{h}_{\ell}}\big/\sum_{j\in[k]}{e^{\bm{h}_{j}}}
.
Consider the function
F:\mathbb{R}_{>0}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
defined as follows

F(p,q)=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\arg\max\left\{{\left(p{\bm{I}}_{k}-q\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)^{1/2}}\,{\bm{g}}+{\left(\alpha{\bm{I}}_{k}-\beta\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)^{1/2}}\,\bm{h}\right\}\neq c(\bm{h})\right),

such that
p{\bm{I}}_{k}-q\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\succ 0
and fixed
\alpha{\bm{I}}_{k}-\beta\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\succ 0
. Then, the following statements are true.

1.

F(p,q)=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\arg\max\left\{\sqrt{p}\cdot{\bm{g}}+\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}\right\}\neq c(\bm{h})\right)
.

2.

For
0<p_{2}<p_{1}
and any
q_{1}<\frac{p_{1}}{k},q_{2}<\frac{p_{2}}{k}
, it holds that
F(p_{2},q_{2})<F(p_{1},q_{1})
.

Proof

Fix any
p>0,q\leq\frac{p}{k}
. Denote
{\bm{T}}:=\left(p{\bm{I}}_{k}-q\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)^{1/2}
and
\bm{S}:=\left(\alpha{\bm{I}}_{k}-\beta\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)^{1/2}
for convenience. It can be checked that
{\bm{T}}:=\left(\sqrt{p}{\bm{I}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{p-qk}-\sqrt{p}}{k}\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)
and
\bm{S}:=\left(\sqrt{\alpha}{\bm{I}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha-\beta k}-\sqrt{\alpha}}{k}\bm{1}_{k}\bm{1}_{k}^{T}\right)
. From these, it follows directly that

\displaystyle F(p,q)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\arg\max\left\{\sqrt{p}\cdot{\bm{g}}+\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}\right\}\neq c(\bm{h})\right).

This shows the first statement.

Next, we show the second statement.
Using the distribution of
c(\bm{h})
and symmetry we have the following chain of equalities:

\displaystyle 1-F(p,q)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\arg\max_{j\in[k]}\left\{\sqrt{p}\cdot{\bm{g}}+\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}\right\}=c(\bm{h})\right\}

\displaystyle=k\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{e^{\bm{h}_{k}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\bm{h}_{j}}}\cdot\mathds{1}{\left\{\arg\max\left\{\sqrt{p}\cdot{\bm{g}}+\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}\right\}=k\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=k\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{e^{\bm{h}_{k}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\bm{h}_{j}}}\cdot\prod_{j\in[k-1]}\mathds{1}{\left\{\sqrt{p}\cdot{\bm{g}}_{j}+\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{j}<\sqrt{p}\cdot{\bm{g}}_{k}+\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{k}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=k\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{e^{\bm{h}_{k}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\bm{h}_{j}}}\cdot\prod_{j\in[k-1]}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{g}}_{j}<{\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{k}-\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{j}}{\sqrt{p}}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=k\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{e^{\bm{h}_{k}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\bm{h}_{j}}}\cdot\prod_{j\in[k-1]}Q\left({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{j}-\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{k}}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right]=:k\cdot G(\sqrt{p})\,,

(H.25)

where in the last line we used the rotational symmetry of the Gaussian distribution:

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{{\bm{g}}_{j}<{\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{k}-\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{j}}{\sqrt{p}}\,|\,\bm{h}_{1},\ldots,\bm{h}_{k}\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left\{{\bm{g}}_{j}>{\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{j}-\sqrt{\alpha}\,\bm{h}_{k}}{\sqrt{p}}\,|\,\bm{h}_{1},\ldots,\bm{h}_{k}\right\}\,,

and the fact that
{\bm{g}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{g}}_{k-1}
are independent.

Next, we will show that the function
{\cal{G}}(\cdot)
defined above is strictly decreasing in
(0,\infty)
. Towards this goal, using
Q^{\prime}(x)=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/2}=-\phi(x)
and using the shorthand

H_{kj}=\bm{h}_{j}-\bm{h}_{k},~j\in[k],

we may compute the derivative of
{\cal{G}}
at any
s>0
as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}{\cal{G}}(s)}{\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k-1]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{e^{\bm{h}_{k}}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{\bm{h}_{j}}}\cdot\phi\Big({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{ki}}{s}\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{ki}}{s^{2}}\cdot\prod_{{j\neq i\in[k-1]}}Q\left({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{kj}}{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k-1]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{H_{kj}}}\cdot\phi\Big({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{ki}}{s}\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{ki}}{s^{2}}\cdot\prod_{{j\neq i\in[k-1]}}Q\left({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{kj}}{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k-1]}\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}}{s^{2}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[H_{ki}\cdot\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right)\right],

(H.26)

where in the last line we have defined

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right):=\frac{1}{1+\sum_{j\in[k-1]}e^{H_{kj}}}\cdot\phi\Big({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{ki}}{s}\Big)\cdot\prod_{{j\neq i\in[k-1]}}Q\left({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{kj}}{s}\right),

Next, we use Gaussian integration by parts (GIBP) to further simplify the expression in (
H.26
).
Fix any
i\in[k-1]
. Then, by (GIBP):

\displaystyle A_{i}

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[H_{ki}\cdot\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right)\right]

(H.27)

\displaystyle\qquad=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[H_{ki}^{2}]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}H_{ki}}\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right)\right]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[k-1]\\
\ell\neq i\end{subarray}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[H_{ki}\cdot H_{k\ell}]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}H_{k\ell}}\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=2\underbrace{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}H_{ki}}\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right)\right]}_{\rm TermI}+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[k-1]\\
\ell\neq i\end{subarray}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}H_{k\ell}}\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right)\right]}_{\rm TermII},

(H.28)

where in the second line, we used the fact that
\bm{h}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},{\bm{I}}_{k})
to compute

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[H_{ki}^{2}]=2,\quad\text{and}~~\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[H_{ki}H_{k\ell}]=1,~\ell\neq i,~\ell\in[k-1].

We now compute the derivatives in (
H.28
). First, for any
\ell\in[k-1],~\ell\neq i
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{A}_{i}\left({\bm{g}}_{k},\{H_{kj}\}_{j\in[k-1]}\right)}{\mathrm{d}H_{k\ell}}=-\frac{e^{H_{k\ell}}}{\left(1+\sum_{j\in[k-1]}e^{H_{kj}}\right)^{2}}\cdot\phi\Big({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{ki}}{s}\Big)\cdot\prod_{{j\neq i\in[k-1]}}Q\left({\bm{g}}_{k}+\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\,H_{kj}}{s}\right)~~=:{\rm TermII(a)_{\ell}}
